# zk.de blog: EN 2013–2016

Ticket: https://github.com/theaustinschool/game-design-map/issues/3
Scope: Every English post on zettelkasten.de dated 2013-01-01 to 2016-12-31, per the year archives and sitemap.xml.
Swept: 2026-11-04

## Conflicts noticed

- create-zettel-from-reading-notes: atomicity of concepts is key <> create-zettel-from-reading-notes: striving for "atomic notes" proved more hindrance than enabler (2025 update)
- create-zettel-from-reading-notes: first try to grasp a whole cluster in a single note <> create-zettel-from-reading-notes: separate concerns into different Zettels
- baseline-zettelkasten-software-reviews: front matter contains the tags <> baseline-zettelkasten-software-reviews: put tags in back matter to keep top lines free
- baseline-zettelkasten-software-reviews: tags in front matter or back matter <> nvalt-zettelkasten-implementation: rely on #hashtags in note content
- how-to-program-yourself-productivity: file name is ID first then title <> nvalt-zettelkasten-implementation: IDs can go at end of file name to fix autocomplete
- zettelkasten-improves-thinking-writing: IDs by counting, insert 57a between adjacent notes <> kinds-of-ties: IDs are timestamps sorted by date
- zettelkasten-improves-thinking-writing: do not sort your stuff, no categories <> write-book-without-even-trying-so-hard: every Zettel gets a place in an outline
- kinds-of-ties: Folgezettel stories only work with few notes / not foundational <> kinds-of-ties: Folgezettel carry a precise scope and useful arbitrary order
- kinds-of-ties: tags are the weaker connection, direct links strongest <> baseline-zettelkasten-software-reviews: notes should both link directly and use keywords for clusters
- why-not-plain-text-wiki: ID-only file names not useful in directory listing (title in file name) <> why-not-plain-text-wiki: ID and title should be separate things, link only to ID
- idea-index-journal-fiction: for non-fiction extract as much as possible <> collectors-fallacy-confession: pick your fights wisely, notes cost up to double reading time
- tinderbox-principles: don't throw anything away <> idea-index-journal-fiction: capturing all quotable passages not worth the effort
- manage-writing-ideas: outline/project notes live in the archive without an ID (2016) <> manage-writing-ideas: 2020 update says assign IDs to outline notes too
- how-many-zettelkasten: a separate topic/project Zettelkasten is fine for a one-off project you won't revisit (Christian) <> how-many-zettelkasten: never use multiple Zettelkasten, always keep old notes (Sascha)
- ease-into-writing: take notes as often as possible, the more you put in the better <> dont-rely-on-source-have-faith-in-yourself: working with a text means being picky, don't aim for full coverage
- ease-into-writing: take notes as often as possible, the more you put in the better <> reading-is-searching: search texts only for specific item types, accept a text may not serve you
- no-categories: the only order is creation date via ID in filename <> manage-writing-ideas: outline files use a "- O" prefix to sort apart from Zettel
- dan-sheffler-workflow: long Zettel is fine, don't chop it to avoid scrolling <> zettelkasten-antifragile: one big Zettel on a topic hinders serendipity
- dan-sheffler-workflow: long Zettel is fine <> luedeckes-zettelkasten-erik-pfeiffer: too-long Zettels were a mistake
- dan-sheffler-workflow: long Zettel is fine <> dan-sheffler-workflow: recommendations say avoid anything longer than a paragraph
- luhmanns-second-zettelkasten: a Zettel doesn't have to be connected, can stand alone forever <> your-first-note: a note not embedded in the web doesn't exist; embed everything
- luhmanns-second-zettelkasten: a Zettel can stand alone <> dan-sheffler-workflow: connect notes heavily
- your-first-note: project Zettelkasten closed to irrelevant input, no unrelated info <> zettelkasten-hubs: don't put in only filtered notes; all arbitrary ideas may be included
- your-first-note: project Zettelkasten closed to irrelevant input <> luhmanns-second-zettelkasten: Zettelkasten can include every topic, no single purpose
- luedeckes-zettelkasten-erik-pfeiffer: Folgezettel are an unnecessary feature <> luhmanns-second-zettelkasten: Folgezettel highly beneficial for very specific needs
- zettelkasten-hubs: ditching Folgezettel loses the variability benefit <> zettelkasten-hubs: Folgezettel unnecessary in a digital Zettelkasten, only a finding aid
- zettelkasten-hubs: Folgezettel don't matter, only need to be able to link <> your-first-note: embed notes via hyperlinks, tags, or note sequences (Folgezettel)
- bibliography-zettelkasten: store full citation redundantly in every Zettel <> luedeckes-zettelkasten-erik-pfeiffer: avoid parallel reference databases / redundancy
- zettelkasten-hubs: Luhmann reading = brief excerpts plus thought list, later split to Zettel <> schopenhauer-reading-thinking: don't stick to the author's exact words
- zettelkasten-hubs: a single Zettel serves as outline and manuscript for a theory <> zettelkasten-antifragile: write separate Zettels for different ideas (atomicity)
- what-is-a-zettel: form a Zettel so another person could use it efficiently <> writing-composing-revising: Zettel are for your eyes only, need not be well-crafted
- search-alone-is-not-enough: link annotations may make sense to you only, which is desired <> what-is-a-zettel: form a Zettel so another person could use it efficiently
- what-is-a-zettel: write as many keywords as you can come up with <> intratextual-tags: keep a threshold, don't fill the archive with everything / nearly every tag in header
- writing-composing-revising: notes of 300-800 words <> what-is-a-zettel: write short, cohesive texts (abstract needed only past 2 paragraphs)
- intratextual-tags: a very large Zettel should not happen, be atomic <> intratextual-tags: splitting tightly coupled content into linked notes can be confusing
- luhmann-folgezettel-truth: Folgezettel is clutter; links alone suffice <> luhmann-folgezettel-truth: (citing Lüdecke) Luhmann's Zettelkasten was mainly about Folgezettel
- search-alone-is-not-enough: keep every chunk in its own Zettel file, single file not convenient <> hot-to-not-get-lost: (Christian) for most books first write all Zettel in a single file
- hot-to-not-get-lost: book overview notes usually deleted afterwards as clutter <> what-is-a-zettel: write overview notes per topic to always have a next step prepared
- one-note-review: assign as many keywords as you can (Sascha, cited) <> one-note-review: keep a limited controlled keyword list; prefer existing keyword
- one-note-review: simple sequential IDs; date in ID is a distraction <> zettel-note-header-in-nvalt: IDs are YYYYMMDDHHmm timestamps
- one-note-review: long notes fine if paragraph-level linking used <> when-start-new-note: longer not better; split notes violating atomicity
- one-note-review: rich-text OneNote fine, plaintext not required <> zettel-note-header-in-nvalt: use plain text, avoid proprietary software
- zettel-note-header-in-nvalt: title optional, ID suffices (usability sugar) <> how-to-write-notes-you-can-understand: title about content is essential, else useless
- one-note-review: title is one word or pithy phrase <> how-to-write-notes-you-can-understand: title states a claim; longer title fine
- one-note-review: parent/child Folgezettel sequences essential <> pirsig-lila: links (not order) form the web; playful re-ordering without IDs is a mistake
- alex-designing-knowledge-management-app: only put reference details in note when really needed <> zettel-note-header-in-nvalt: minimal Zettel includes list of references
- zettel-note-header-in-nvalt: tags at bottom (plain text) <> zettel-note-header-in-nvalt: tags in header (MultiMarkdown)

New tags used: `other:abstract`, `other:anatomy`, `other:archive`, `other:body`, `other:capture`, `other:categories`, `other:collector`, `other:definition`, `other:emergence`, `other:essence`, `other:expectations`, `other:feedback`, `other:first-note`, `other:fragments`, `other:gaps`, `other:images`, `other:item-types`, `other:knowledge`, `other:layout`, `other:media`, `other:metadata`, `other:mindset`, `other:non-zettel`, `other:note-types`, `other:organization`, `other:personal`, `other:principles`, `other:purpose`, `other:relevance`, `other:scale`, `other:scope`, `other:self-contained`, `other:single-archive`, `other:structure`, `other:surprise`, `other:system`, `other:templates`, `other:visual`.

## Known gaps

- Reader comments. Every post embeds a Disqus thread (shortname `christiantietze`) loaded by JavaScript. The sweep used curl, so no comment text was read, including any replies by Christian Tietze or Sascha Fast. A browser pass over each post's `#comments` would close this.
- Link-post targets. 15 posts in range are link posts that point at external pages (Micah Joel, Dan Sheffler, Daniel Lüdecke, Farnam Street, Pirsig via a third-party page, and others). Only the zettelkasten.de permalink commentary was read. The external pages are not zettelkasten.de content.
- Images and figures. Screenshots and diagrams were not read. Claims carried only by an image (for example, a pictured note layout) are missing.
- Republished posts. 28 posts in range carry the footer "originally appeared on christiantietze.de". Only the zettelkasten.de copy was read. Differences from the original publication were not checked.
- Members-only material. The Members Area and paid course "The Perfect Zettel" are linked from every page and were not accessed.
- German posts. None of the 105 in-range English-archive pages is German (all serve `lang="en"`). German posts under `/de/posts/` belong to ticket #6 and were not enumerated here.
- Later editorial updates. Some 2013-2016 posts carry update notes added years later (for example a 2020 note in "Where Should My Writing Ideas Go?" and a 2025 note in "Create Zettel from Reading Notes"). The `date` column gives the original publication date shown on the page. Rows from those update notes say so in the claim text, but update text was not systematically separated from original text.
- Conflict detection was done per reading batch (5 batches of 21 posts). Conflicts between posts in different batches can be missing from Conflicts noticed.
- Quote verification. Every quote was script-checked as an exact substring (whitespace-normalized) of the live page text, re-fetched on 2026-11-04 and found identical to the first fetch. Claim wording and author attribution inside posts with co-author comments ("Sascha's Comment", "Christian's Comment") were spot-checked, not checked row by row.
